.navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar print{.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:none!important}}</style></span><div class=\"navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini\"><ul><li class=\"nv-view\"><a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" class=\"nv-edit\"><a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:EditPage/Template:Politics_of_Algeria\" title=\"Special:EditPage/Template:Politics of Algeria\"><abbr title=\"Edit this template\">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>\n\n<p id=\"mwCA\"><a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algeria\" title=\"Algeria\" id=\"mwCQ\">Algeria</a>, as of 2025, is divided into 69 <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilaya\" title=\"Wilaya\" id=\"mwCg\">wilayas</a> (<a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Province\" title=\"Province\" id=\"mwCw\">provinces</a>).<sup about=\"#mwt16\" class=\"mw-ref reference\" id=\"cite_ref-1\" rel=\"dc:references\" typeof=\"mw:Extension/ref\" data-mw=\"{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-1&quot;}}\"><a href=\"#cite_note-1\" id=\"mwDA\"><span class=\"mw-reflink-text\" id=\"mwDQ\"><span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwDg\">[</span>1<span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwDw\">]</span></span></a></sup> Prior to November 16, 2025, there were 58 provinces. The 69 provinces are divided into 1,541 <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baladiyah\" title=\"Baladiyah\" class=\"mw-redirect\" id=\"mwEA\">baladiyahs</a> (<a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_of_Algeria\" title=\"Municipalities of Algeria\" class=\"mw-redirect\" id=\"mwEQ\">municipalities</a>). The name of a province is always that of its capital city. </p>\n\n<p id=\"mwEg\">According to the Algerian constitution, a wilaya is a territorial collectivity enjoying economic and diplomatic freedom, the APW, or <i id=\"mwEw\">Popular Provincial Parliament/Provincial Popular Parliament</i> (the <i id=\"mwFA\"><b id=\"mwFQ\">A</b>ssemblée <b id=\"mwFg\">P</b>opulaire <b id=\"mwFw\">W</b>ilayale</i>, in French) is the political entity governing a province, directed by the <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wali_(administrative_title)\" title=\"Wali (administrative title)\" class=\"mw-redirect\" id=\"mwGA\"><i id=\"mwGQ\">Wali</i></a> (<a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor\" title=\"Governor\" id=\"mwGg\">Governor</a>), who is chosen by the <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_President\" title=\"Algerian President\" class=\"mw-redirect\" id=\"mwGw\">Algerian President</a> to handle the APW's decisions, the APW also has a president, who is elected by the members of the APW, which Algerians elect.</p>\n\n<p id=\"mwHA\">On November 16, 2025, the announcement was made of 11 new provinces in the <i id=\"mwHQ\"><a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hautes_Plaines\" title=\"Hautes Plaines\" id=\"mwHg\">Hautes Plaines</a></i> and the south of the country. These 11 new provinces are Aflou, Barika, Ksar Chellala, Messaad, Aïn Oussera, Boussaâda, El Abiodh Sidi Cheikh, El Kantara, Bir El Ater, Ksar El Boukhari, and El Aricha. Creating these new provinces made the provinces of Laghouat, Batna, Tiaret, Djelfa, M'sila, El Bayadh, Biskra, Médéa, and Tlemcen, smaller.<sup about=\"#mwt19\" class=\"mw-ref reference\" id=\"cite_ref-2\" rel=\"dc:references\" typeof=\"mw:Extension/ref\" data-mw=\"{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-2&quot;}}\"><a href=\"#cite_note-2\" id=\"mwHw\"><span class=\"mw-reflink-text\" id=\"mwIA\"><span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwIQ\">[</span>2<span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwIg\">]</span></span></a></sup></p>\n\n<meta property=\"mw:PageProp/toc\" updated per Law N° 26-06, April 4, 2026.</figcaption></figure>\n<p id=\"mwKg\">By 1984 the number of Algerian provinces was fixed at 48 and established the list of municipalities or \"communes\" attached to each province. In 2019, 10 new provinces were added.<sup about=\"#mwt24\" class=\"mw-ref reference\" id=\"cite_ref-3\" rel=\"dc:references\" typeof=\"mw:Extension/ref\" data-mw=\"{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-3&quot;}}\"><a href=\"#cite_note-3\" id=\"mwKw\"><span class=\"mw-reflink-text\" id=\"mwLA\"><span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwLQ\">[</span>3<span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwLg\">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about=\"#mwt27\" class=\"mw-ref reference\" id=\"cite_ref-4\" rel=\"dc:references\" typeof=\"mw:Extension/ref\" data-mw=\"{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-4&quot;}}\"><a href=\"#cite_note-4\" id=\"mwLw\"><span class=\"mw-reflink-text\" id=\"mwMA\"><span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwMQ\">[</span>4<span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwMg\">]</span></span></a></sup></p>\n\n<p id=\"mwMw\">The province numbers are the first 31 provinces (see the second section) in <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language\" title=\"Arabic language\" class=\"mw-redirect\" id=\"mwNA\">Arabic</a> alphabetical order, after the adding of 17 more provinces in 1983 and 10 more in 2019, the old numbering was kept and the 27 provinces created since 1984 have been assigned codes from 32 to 58, in Arabic <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alphabetical_order\" title=\"Alphabetical order\" id=\"mwNQ\">alphabetical order</a>.</p>\n\n<p id=\"mwNg\">The following table presents the list of provinces, showing for each its numerical code, its name (which is in every case the same as the name of the capital city of the province), the number of districts (\"dairas\"), the number of municipalities, its area, and its population.</p>\n<div style=\"clear:both;\" class=\"\" about=\"#mwt28\" typeof=\"mw:Transclusion\" id=\"mwNw\" data-mw=\"{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;clear&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Clear&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}\"></div>\n<span class=\"mw-empty-elt\" about=\"#mwt29\" typeof=\"mw:Transclusion\" id=\"mwOA\" data-mw=\"{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;update aria-labelledby=\"2019–2025\"><div class=\"mw-heading mw-heading2\"><h2 id=\"2019–2025\"><span id=\"2019.E2.80.932025\" typeof=\"mw:FallbackId\"></span>2019–2025</h2><span class=\"mw-editsection\"><span class=\"mw-editsection-bracket\">[</span><a href=\"/w/index.php?title=Provinces_of_Algeria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3\" title=\"Edit section: 2019–2025\"><span>edit</span></a><span class=\"mw-editsection-bracket\">]</span></span></div>\n<p id=\"mwA1s\">On 26 November 2019, the <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" provinces in the south of Algeria into smaller ones.<sup about=\"#mwt104\" class=\"mw-ref reference\" id=\"cite_ref-5\" rel=\"dc:references\" typeof=\"mw:Extension/ref\" data-mw=\"{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-5&quot;}}\"><a href=\"#cite_note-5\" id=\"mwA10\"><span class=\"mw-reflink-text\" id=\"mwA14\"><span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwA18\">[</span>5<span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwA2A\">]</span></span></a></sup> Thus, the following provinces have been added on December 18, 2019:</p>\n<ul id=\"mwA2E\"><li id=\"mwA2I\"><a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bordj_Badji_Mokhtar_Province\" title=\"Bordj Badji Mokhtar Province\" Province</a></li>\n<li id=\"mwA2Y\"><a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Djanet_Province\" title=\"Djanet Province\" id=\"mwA2c\">Djanet Province</a></li>\n<li id=\"mwA2g\"><a href=\"/w/index.php?title=Provinces_of_Algeria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6\" title=\"Edit section: 1957–1974\"><span>edit</span></a><span id=\"mwA6s\"></figcaption></figure>\n<p id=\"mwA6w\">Immediately after independence, Algeria retained its 15 former French départements, which were renamed <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilaya\" title=\"Wilaya\" id=\"mwA60\">wilayas</a> (provinces) in 1968, for the most part, with some name changes:</p>\n\n<ul id=\"mwA64\"><li id=\"mwA68\">8A-El Wahat (Currently Ouargla, formerly Oasis)</li>\n<li id=\"mwA7A\">8B-Saoura (Currently Béchar)</li>\n<li id=\"mwA7E\">9A-Alger (Algiers)</li>\n<li id=\"mwA7I\">9B-Batna</li>\n<li id=\"mwA7M\">9C-Annaba (Formerly Bône, <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language\" title=\"English language\" id=\"mwA7Q\">English</a>: Bona)</li>\n<li id=\"mwA7U\">9D-Constantine</li>\n<li id=\"mwA7Y\">9E-Médéa</li>\n<li id=\"mwA7c\">9F-Mostaganem</li>\n<li id=\"mwA7g\">9G-Oran</li>\n<li id=\"mwA7k\">9H-Orléansville (Then El Asnam, now Chlef)</li>\n<li id=\"mwA7o\">9J-Sétif</li>\n<li id=\"mwA7s\">9K-Tiaret</li>\n<li id=\"mwA7w\">9L-Tizi-Ouzou</li>\n<li id=\"mwA70\">9M-Tlemcen</li>\n<li id=\"mwA74\">9R-Saïda</li></ul>\n\n</section><section data-mw-section-id=\"7\" id=\"mwA78\" aria-labelledby=\"1954–1962\"><div class=\"mw-heading mw-heading2\"><h2 id=\"1954–1962\"><span id=\"1954.E2.80.931962\" typeof=\"mw:FallbackId\"></span>1954–1962</h2><span class=\"mw-editsection\"><span class=\"mw-editsection-bracket\">[</span><a href=\"/w/index.php?title=Provinces_of_Algeria&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7\" title=\"Edit section: 1954–1962\"><span>edit</span></a><span class=\"mw-editsection-bracket\">]</span></span></div>\n<p id=\"mwA8A\">During the <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algerian_War_of_Independence\" title=\"Algerian War of Independence\" class=\"mw-redirect\" id=\"mwA8E\">Algerian War of Independence</a>, the <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Liberation_Front_(Algeria)\" title=\"National Liberation Front (Algeria)\" id=\"mwA8I\">FLN</a> adopted an organizational system divided by 6 numbered <i id=\"mwA8M\">wilayas</i>:</p>\n\n<ol id=\"mwA8Q\"><li id=\"mwA8U\">Aurès</li>\n<li id=\"mwA8Y\">Constantine</li>\n<li id=\"mwA8c\">Kabylie</li>\n<li id=\"mwA8g\">Algiers</li>\n<li id=\"mwA8k\">Oran</li>\n<li id=\"mwA8o\">Sahara</li></ol>\n\n</section><section data-mw-section-id=\"8\" id=\"mwA8s\"
